<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Four real Banach spaces defined by bounded tree potentials satisfy the averaged asymptotic midpoint bound $`\widehat\delta(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1`$ for $`0\lt t\lt 1`$, while none admits an asymptotically uniformly convex (AUC) renorming. On finite-height trees, the globally constrained norm equals the least additive cost of a Hilbert vector and root paths. The quadratic path and segment-start outer Hilbert sums are reflexive and asymptotically midpoint uniformly convex, and admit no equivalent AUC norm.

- **claim-four-spaces-midpoint-no-auc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-four)): Each of the four completed spaces $X_K$ is a separable infinite-dimensional real Banach space. For $K\in\{K_{\rm s},K_{\rm p},K_{\rm a},K_{\rm g}\}$ and $0<t<1$, $$\label{eq:common-bound} \widehat\delta_{X_K}(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1.$$ None of these spaces admits an equivalent AUC norm.
  - Explanation: The four bounded-potential spaces share one quadratic averaged midpoint bound on the stated range of radii and none has an equivalent AUC norm.
- **claim-paired-inward-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-constrained-paired)): For any of the four norms, any $x$ supported in a finite initial $D$, any $y\in F_D$, and $t>0$, one has $$\label{eq:pair-inequality} \frac{\\|x+ty\\|_K+\\|x-ty\\|_K}{2} \ge \sqrt{1-\theta^2}\,\\|x\\|_K +\frac{t\theta}{2}\\|y\\|_K \qquad(0\le\theta\le\tfrac12).$$
  - Explanation: Pairing the two inward clipped tests on the tail gives a lower bound for the average of the two endpoint norms in terms of head norm and tail norm.
- **claim-bounded-tree-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-tree)): Let $X$ be a real Banach space. Suppose $0<m\le M<\infty$ and $c>0$, and, for every integer $h\ge1$, $X$ contains a tree $(p_s)_{s\in\mathbb N^{\le h}}$ with $m\le\\|p_s\\|\le M$. For every nonterminal $s$, suppose the child increments $d_{s,n}=p_{s^\frown n}-p_s$ are weakly null as $n\to\infty$ and $\\|d_{s,n}\\|\ge c$. If $N$ is an equivalent norm with $\alpha\\|\cdot\\|\le N\le\beta\\|\cdot\\|$ for constants $0<\alpha\le\beta<\infty$, then $\overline\delta_N(\alpha c/(2\beta M))=0$.
  - Explanation: Bounded trees with weakly null child increments bounded away from zero force the one-sided modulus to vanish at an explicit scale under every equivalent norm.
- **claim-free-root-modulus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-free-root-modulus)): The real Banach space $X_{\mathrm e}$ defined by <a href="#fr:tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:tests">[fr:tests]</a> and <a href="#fr:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:norm">[fr:norm]</a> satisfies $$\widehat\delta_{\\|\cdot\\|_{\mathrm e}}(t) \ge\frac{\sqrt{1+t^2/576}-1}{4}\qquad(0<t<1). \label{fr:modulus}$$ It admits no equivalent AUC norm. More precisely, if $\alpha\\|\cdot\\|_{\mathrm e}\le N\le\beta\\|\cdot\\|_{\mathrm e}$, where $0<\alpha\le\beta<\infty$, then $\overline\delta_N(\alpha/(4\beta))=0$.
  - Explanation: The edge-coordinate space with free root potential has a positive averaged midpoint modulus and an explicit AUC-renorming obstruction scale.
- **claim-linear-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-linear-duality)): The space $X_n=(H_n,L_n)$ is complete and $$\frac{\\|x\\|_2}{\sqrt{n+1}}\le L_n(x)\le\\|x\\|_2, \qquad L_n(x)=\sup_{p_n(f)\le1}\langle f,x\rangle.$$ Every coordinate satisfies $\|x_s\|\le L_n(x)$. Its continuous dual is $H_n$, under the Hilbert pairing, with norm $p_n$. In particular $L_n$ is exactly the global test norm on $T_n$.
  - Explanation: On a finite-height tree the least additive cost of a Hilbert vector plus root paths equals the dual global test norm.
- **claim-linear-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-linear-geometry)): The space $X$ is reflexive, has separable dual, and its dual is the Hilbert sum of the spaces $(H_n,p_n)$, with pairing $$f(x)=\sum_n\langle f_n,x_n\rangle, \qquad \\|f\\|_*^2=\sum_np_n(f_n)^2.$$ Every coordinate vector $e_{n,s}$ and every root path $b_{n,s}$ has norm one. For fixed $n$ and $\|s\|<n$, the children $(e_{n,s^\frown j})_{j\ge1}$ are weakly null. If $\alpha\\|\cdot\\|\le N\le\beta\\|\cdot\\|$, with $0<\alpha\le\beta$, then $\overline\delta_N(t)=0$ for every $0<t\le\alpha/\beta$. In particular no equivalent norm on $X$ is asymptotically uniformly convex.
  - Explanation: The outer Hilbert sum is reflexive with separable dual, has weakly null branching, and admits no equivalent AUC norm.
- **claim-linear-inward-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-inward)): Let $A$ be a finite head. Suppose $h,g\in X^*$ have norms at most one and $P_Ah=h$. There are $r_1,r_2\in X^*$, vanishing on $A$, such that $$r_1-r_2=(I-P_A)g,\qquad \\|r_l\\|_*\le2, \qquad \\|\lambda h+\mu r_l\\|_*^2\le\lambda^2+4\mu^2$$ for $l=1,2$ and $\lambda,\mu\ge0$.
  - Explanation: A dual test off a finite head splits into two inward pieces with controlled combined norm against the head test.
- **claim-linear-head-tail-clipping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-head-tail-clipping)): If $x,y\in X$, $P_Ax=x$ and $P_Ay=0$ for a finite head $A$, then, for every $c>0$, $$\max\{\\|x+y\\|,\\|x-y\\|\} \ge\frac{\\|x\\|+c\\|y\\|/2}{\sqrt{1+4c^2}}. \label{eq:linear-clipping-family}$$ In particular the left side is at least $\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+\\|y\\|^2/16}$.
  - Explanation: Clipping gives a family of lower bounds for the larger endpoint norm when the displacement vanishes on the head.
- **claim-linear-weak-sequence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-weak-sequence)): Let $x\in X$ and let $(y_j)$ be a bounded weakly null sequence in $X$. If $L=\liminf_j\\|y_j\\|$, then $$\liminf_j\max\{\\|x+y_j\\|,\\|x-y_j\\|\} \ge\sqrt{\\|x\\|^2+L^2/16}.$$
  - Explanation: The clipping bound persists asymptotically along bounded weakly null sequences around an arbitrary center.
- **claim-linear-positive-monotonicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-positive-monotonicity)): If $0\le\nu\le\mu$ in $\ell_1(U)$, then $L_U(B_U\nu)\le L_U(B_U\mu)$.
  - Explanation: The forest path norm is monotone in nonnegative path coefficients.
- **claim-linear-positive-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-positive-tail)): Let $C=6(\sqrt2+1)$. In one component, if $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, and $L_n(x\pm y)\le1$, then $$\label{eq:linear-positive-unit} L_n(y)\le C\sqrt{1-L_n(x)}.$$ For arbitrary $x,y$ satisfying $P_Ax=x$ and $P_Ay=0$, writing $\beta=\max\{L_n(x+y),L_n(x-y)\}$ and $r=L_n(x)$, one has $$\label{eq:linear-positive-scaled} L_n(y)^2\le C^2\beta(\beta-r)\le C^2(\beta^2-r^2).$$ Consequently, for a finite head projection $P$ on $X$, $$\label{eq:linear-positive-outer} \\|y\\|^2\le C^2\bigl(\\|x+y\\|^2+\\|x-y\\|^2-2\\|x\\|^2\bigr) \quad(Px=x,\ Py=0).$$
  - Explanation: The tail of the displacement is bounded by the square root of the center deficit using the one-sign structure of path mass.
- **claim-linear-subtree-quota** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-subtree-quota)): On a forest of finite height, let $\mu,\nu$ be nonnegative finitely supported endpoint masses. For a vertex $v$, let $K_v$ be its descendant set, and put $G(v)=\mu(K_v)$, $W(v)=\nu(K_v)$. There is $0\le\rho\le\mu$ such that $$\rho(K_v)\ge\min\{G(v),W(v)\}\quad\hbox{for every }v, \qquad \\|\rho\\|_1\le\\|\nu\\|_1.$$ The last inequality also holds separately in each tree of the forest.
  - Explanation: A bottom-up selection meets subtree quotas with total mass controlled by the target measure.
- **claim-linear-bottom-up-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-bottom-up-tail)): Suppose $0\le\varepsilon\le1$, $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, $L_n(x\pm y)\le1$, and $L_n(x)\ge1-\varepsilon$. Then $$\label{eq:linear-bottom-up-explicit} L_n(y)\le3\sqrt{2\varepsilon}+8\varepsilon.$$ Moreover the following uniform implication holds on $X$: for any sequence of finite head projections $P_j$ and vectors satisfying $$P_jx_j=x_j,\quad P_jy_j=0,\quad \\|x_j\pm y_j\\|\le1,\quad\\|x_j\\|\longrightarrow1,$$ one has $\\|y_j\\|\longrightarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Near-unit centers force the tail to be small, with an explicit bound and a uniform implication on the outer sum.
- **claim-linear-trimming** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-trimming-242)): For a finite head projection $P$ on $X$, $$\label{eq:linear-trimming-242} \\|y\\|^2\le242\bigl(\\|x+y\\|^2+\\|x-y\\|^2-2\\|x\\|^2\bigr) \qquad(Px=x,\ Py=0).$$ The same assertion, with $L_n$ in place of $\\|\cdot\\|$, holds in each component.
  - Explanation: Quota removal yields a homogeneous squared tail estimate.
- **claim-linear-fresh-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-fresh-layer-400)): Let $A\subseteq A'$ be finite ancestor-closed subsets of the disjoint union of the $T_n$, and put $E=A'\setminus A$. If $P_Ax=x$ and $P_{A'}y=y$, then $$\label{eq:linear-fresh-layer-400} \\|P_Ey\\|^2\le400\left( \frac{\\|x+y\\|^2+\\|x-y\\|^2}{2}-\\|x\\|^2\right).$$ There is no condition on $P_Ay$.
  - Explanation: Stopping at excess wrong-sign mass bounds a fresh layer with no condition on the old window.
- **claim-linear-predictable-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-predictable-energy)): Let $x_0,\ldots,x_m$ be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Let $P_0$ be a fixed finite initial set, and, for $1\le i\le m$, let $P_i$ be a finite initial set measurable with respect to $\mathcal F_{i-1}$. Suppose, on every history, that $$P_{i-1}\subseteq P_i\quad(1\le i\le m),\qquad \operatorname{supp}x_i\subseteq P_i\quad(0\le i\le m),\qquad \\|x_m\\|\le C.$$ Then, for $\Delta_i=P_i\setminus P_{i-1}$, $$\label{eq:linear-energy-495} \sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E\\|R_{\Delta_i}x_i\\|^2\le495C^2.$$
  - Explanation: Expected squared norms on successively exposed predictable layers of a dyadic martingale sum to a bounded multiple of the terminal bound.
- **claim-linear-mass-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-linear-mass-removal)): Let $S\subseteq T\subset T_n$ be finite initial sets, let $z^+,z^-\in H_n$, and put $z=(z^++z^-)/2$. Put $U=T\setminus S$ and $$g=\tfrac12\big(V_T(z^+)+V_T(z^-)\big)-V_S(z).$$ Then $g\ge0$ and, for either sign, $$\label{eq:linear-mass-removal} L_n(R_Uz^\pm)\le2\\|R_Uz\\|_2+4g.$$
  - Explanation: Excess positive mass can be removed so the child layer is bounded by Hilbert norm plus the variation gain.
- **claim-linear-variation-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-variation-energy)): Under all hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:linear-predictable-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:linear-predictable-energy">16</a>, $$\label{eq:linear-energy-252} \sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E\\|R_{\Delta_i}x_i\\|^2\le252C^2.$$ In particular the bound is $1008$ when $\\|x_m\\|\le2$.
  - Explanation: Variation telescoping gives a sharper bound on the same layer energy.
- **claim-linear-sign-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-sign-energy)): There is an absolute constant $K$ with the following property. Let $x_t=\mathbb E_t x_m$, $0\le t\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. For $0\le t<m$, let $A_t\subseteq D_t$ be finite initial sets measurable with respect to $\mathcal F_t$, and suppose $D_t\subseteq A_{t+1}$ whenever $t+1<m$. Put $U_t=D_t\setminus A_t$. Then $$\label{eq:linear-sign-energy} \sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_{t+1}\\|^2 \le K\left(\mathbb E\\|x_m\\|^2+ \sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_t\\|^2\right).$$ No support condition on $x_t$ is imposed.
  - Explanation: For adaptive windows with parent leakage, the child energy is bounded by an absolute constant times terminal and parent energies.
- **claim-linear-leakage-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-leakage-energy)): Let $W_i=\mathbb E_iW_m$, $0\le i\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Let $A_0\subseteq\cdots\subseteq A_m$ be fixed finite initial sets and put $\Delta_i=A_i\setminus A_{i-1}$. Then $$\label{eq:linear-leakage-1024} \sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E\\|R_{\Delta_i}W_i\\|^2 \le1024\left(\mathbb E\\|W_m\\|^2+ \sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E\\|R_{\Delta_i}W_{i-1}\\|^2\right).$$
  - Explanation: For fixed heads, child window energy is bounded by terminal energy plus actual parent leakage.
- **claim-quadratic-construction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-construction)): The spaces $Z_n$ and $X_{\mathrm q}$ are Banach and reflexive, and finite node support is dense in $X_{\mathrm q}$. For $z\in H_n$, $$\label{qp:comparison} \frac{\\|z\\|_2}{\sqrt{n+2}}\le \|z\|_n\le\\|z\\|_2, \qquad \|z_t\|\le\sqrt2\,\|z\|_n.$$ In particular $1/\sqrt2\le \|p_t\|_n,\|e_t\|_n\le1$, and $\|e_o\|_n=1/\sqrt2$. For $f\in\ell_2(T_n)$ the dual norm is $$\label{qp:dual} \|f\|_{n,*}= \left(\\|f\\|_2^2+ \sup_{t\in T_n}\left\|\sum_{s\preceq t}f_s\right\|^2\right)^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic path component spaces and their outer sum are reflexive Banach spaces with finite support dense, with explicit norm comparisons and dual norm.
- **claim-quadratic-head-fold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-head)): The operator $R_A$ which sends the mass $\lambda_t$ to $r(t)$ and discards it when $r(t)=\perp$ is contractive on $L$. Moreover $$P_AJ=JR_A,\qquad \\|P_A\\|\le1,\qquad \\|Q_A\\|\le2, \qquad \|Q_AJ_n\lambda\|_n\le2\\|\lambda\\|_1.$$ Here the last formula is in a single component. In particular the path $p_t-p_{r(t)}$ outside the head has norm at most two.
  - Explanation: Folding mass onto the head is contractive and gives the projection bounds.
- **claim-quadratic-no-auc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-no-auc)): No equivalent norm on $X_{\mathrm q}$ is asymptotically uniformly convex. More precisely, if $a\\|z\\|_{\mathrm q}\le N(z)\le b\\|z\\|_{\mathrm q}$, with $0<a\le b<\infty$, then $\overline\delta_N(t_0)=0$ at $t_0=a/(\sqrt2 b)$.
  - Explanation: No equivalent norm on the quadratic path space is AUC; the modulus vanishes at an explicit scale.
- **claim-quadratic-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-energy)): Let $$C=\frac12\sum_{\epsilon\in\{+,-\}} (\\|h^\epsilon\\|_H^2+\\|\lambda^\epsilon\\|_L^2), \qquad E=C-\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2.$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} \\|Q\bar h\\|_H^2+\\|\widetilde h\\|_H^2+ \sum_n(M_n^2-q_n^2)&\le E,\label{qp:energy-mean}\\ \tfrac12(\\|Qh^+\\|_H^2+\\|Qh^-\\|_H^2)+ \sum_n d_n^2&\le E.\label{qp:energy-endpoints} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The loss from averaging and restricting representations controls Hilbert and mass terms.
- **claim-quadratic-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-flow)): Let $O=T_n\setminus A_n$, let $\mathcal R$ be the roots of its connected subtrees, and suppose $V:O\to[0,\infty)$ satisfies $$V(t)\ge\sum_{c\text{ child of }t}V(c),\qquad B:=\sum_{c\in\mathcal R}V(c)<\infty.$$ For any signs $\varepsilon_t$ constant on each connected subtree of $O$, the vector $\varepsilon_tV(t)$ on $O$, extended by zero on $A_n$, has norm at most $2B$ in $Z_n$. The pointwise minimum of two arrays satisfying the child inequality also satisfies it.
  - Explanation: Stopped flows on the outside forest have norm bounded by their initial mass, independent of height and branching.
- **claim-quadratic-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-quadratic-tail)): Let $A$ be a finite ancestral head in $X_{\mathrm q}$ and let $P_Ax=x$. For every $y\in X_{\mathrm q}$ and $R\ge0$, $$\label{qp:tail-eight} \\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm q},\ \\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le R \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|Q_Ay\\|_{\mathrm q}\le8\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2}.$$ The displacement $y$ need not vanish on the head.
  - Explanation: The tail of an arbitrary displacement is bounded by a multiple of the square root of the radius deficit.
- **claim-quadratic-lens-eight** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-lens-eight)): If $x\in X_{\mathrm q}$ and an infinite sequence $(y_j)$ satisfies $\\|x\pm y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\le R$ and $\\|y_i-y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\ge\varepsilon>0$ for $i\ne j$, then $$\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2+\frac{\varepsilon^2}{256}\le R^2.$$
  - Explanation: A separated infinite sequence in a symmetric lens forces a quantitative drop in the center norm.
- **claim-quadratic-symmetric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-symmetric-twelve)): For a finite ancestral head $A$, $P_Ax=x$, and $P_Ay=0$, $$\label{qp:symmetric-144} \\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2+\frac{\\|y\\|_{\mathrm q}^2}{144} \le\frac{\\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm q}^2+\\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm q}^2}{2}.$$
  - Explanation: A symmetric clipping estimate keeps the average of squared endpoint norms.
- **claim-quadratic-lens-twelve** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-lens-twelve)): If $\\|x\pm y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\le r$ and $\\|y_i-y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\ge2d>0$ for $i\ne j$ in an infinite sequence, then $$\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2+d^2/144\le r^2.$$
  - Explanation: The symmetric estimate yields a separated-lens inequality.
- **claim-quadratic-tail-seven** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-tail-seven)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:quadratic-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:quadratic-tail">26</a>, $$\\|Q_Ay\\|_{\mathrm q}\le7\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2}.$$
  - Explanation: A stopping argument improves the tail coefficient.
- **claim-quadratic-lens-seven** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-lens-seven)): For $0<c\le1$ put $\delta=c^2/2000$. If $\\|x\pm y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\le1$ and $\\|y_i-y_j\\|_{\mathrm q}\ge2c$ for all distinct indices of an infinite sequence, then $\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}\le1-\delta$.
  - Explanation: Separated lens points force the center norm below one by an explicit margin.
- **claim-quadratic-tail-nine** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-tail-nine)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:quadratic-tail" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:quadratic-tail">26</a>, $$\\|Q_Ay\\|_{\mathrm q}\le9\sqrt{R^2-\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}^2}.$$
  - Explanation: Majority-sign pruning gives another tail bound for the same setting.
- **claim-quadratic-lens-nine** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-lens-nine)): For every $\rho>0$, choose $0<\gamma<1/2$ such that $$9\sqrt{(1+\gamma)^2-(1-2\gamma)^2}<\rho/3.$$ If $\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}\ge1-\gamma$, its symmetric lens $\{y:\\|x\pm y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le1\}$ has no infinite $\rho$-separated subset.
  - Explanation: Near-unit centers have lenses with no infinite separated subset.
- **claim-quadratic-relative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-relative-seventytwo)): For every nonzero $x\in X_{\mathrm q}$ and $0<\delta\le1$ there is a finite ancestral head $A$, depending only on $x,\delta$, such that for every $u\in X_{\mathrm q}$, $$\\|x+u\\|_{\mathrm q},\ \\|x-u\\|_{\mathrm q} \le(1+\delta)\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|Q_Au\\|_{\mathrm q}\le72\sqrt\delta\,\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}.$$
  - Explanation: One head chosen from the center and a relative excess controls all admissible displacements.
- **claim-quadratic-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-rec-component)): Let $A\subset T_n$ be a finite nonempty ancestral head, with $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, and $\|x\|_n=1$. If $0\le e\le1$ and $\|x\pm y\|_n\le1+e$, then $$\label{qp:rec:component-bound} \|y\|_n\le(6+\sqrt2)\sqrt{(1+e)^2-1}\le13\sqrt e.$$
  - Explanation: A single component displacement bound in terms of the relative endpoint excess.
- **claim-quadratic-finite-center** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-rec-finite-center)): Let $x\in X_{\mathrm q}$ be a unit vector with finite coordinate support. For each $n$ with $x_n\ne0$, choose a finite nonempty ancestor-closed $A_n\subset T_n$ containing its support, and set $$Y_x=\{y\in X_{\mathrm q}:P_{A_n}y_n=0 \text{ whenever }x_n\ne0\}.$$ Then $Y_x$ is a closed finite-codimensional subspace. If $0<e\le1/8$, $y\in Y_x$, and $\\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm q},\\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le1+e$, then $$\label{qp:rec:outer-bound} \\|y\\|_{\mathrm q}^2\le\frac{519}{2}e^{1/3}, \qquad \\|y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le17e^{1/6}.$$
  - Explanation: For finitely supported unit centers the Hilbert sum has a finite-codimensional subspace on which displacements are small when the excess is small.
- **claim-quadratic-all-centers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-qp-rec-all-centers)): For every $x\in X_{\mathrm q}$ with $\\|x\\|_{\mathrm q}=1$ and $0<e\le1/16$, there is a closed finite-codimensional subspace $Y\subset X_{\mathrm q}$ such that $$\label{qp:rec:complete-bound} y\in Y,\quad \\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm q},\\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le1+e \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|y\\|_{\mathrm q}\le17(2e)^{1/6}.$$ In particular, for every $t>0$ there is an $e_t>0$, independent of the unit center $x$, for which some closed finite-codimensional $Y$ satisfies $$\inf_{u\in S_Y} \max\{\\|x+tu\\|_{\mathrm q},\\|x-tu\\|_{\mathrm q}\}\ge1+e_t.$$ Here $S_Y=\{u\in Y:\\|u\\|_{\mathrm q}=1\}$. Thus the maximum asymptotic midpoint modulus is positive at every positive radius.
  - Explanation: The estimate extends to all unit centers, giving positive maximum midpoint modulus at every positive radius.
- **claim-segment-cost-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-s-cost-properties)): The costs are monotone, positively homogeneous and subadditive. On $\mathcal T_n$ they satisfy $$\label{sm:s:cost-comparisons} \\|m\\|_2\le M_n(m) \le\sum_{d=0}^{n}\\|m\|_{\mathbb N^d}\\|_2 \le\sqrt{n+1}\,\\|m\\|_2, \qquad \sum_{t\preceq v}m_t\le M_n(m).$$ For nonnegative forest arrays of finite total cost, $$\label{sm:s:positive-energy} M(m+r)^2\ge M(m)^2+M(r)^2.$$
  - Explanation: The start-mass costs are monotone, homogeneous, subadditive and satisfy comparisons and a positive energy inequality.
- **claim-segment-space** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-s-space)): On $\ell_2(\mathcal T_n)$ the formula $$\\|x\\|_{S,n}=\inf\{M_n(m(a)):Pa=x\}$$ defines a complete reflexive norm, with $$\label{sm:s:norm-comparisons} \frac{\\|x\\|_2}{n+1}\le\\|x\\|_{S,n} \le\sqrt{n+1}\,\\|x\\|_2.$$ Consequently $$X_S=\Bigl(\bigoplus_{n\ge1}(\ell_2(\mathcal T_n),\\|\cdot\\|_{S,n}) \Bigr)_{\ell_2}$$ is a reflexive Banach space. Write $\\|\cdot\\|_S$ for its norm. Equivalently, $\\|x\\|_S$ is the infimum of $M(m(a))$ over all forest representations of $x$. Finite-support vectors are dense.
  - Explanation: The segment-start quotient formula defines a complete reflexive norm equivalent to the Hilbert norm on each component.
- **claim-segment-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-s-coordinates)): For every node $v$ and $x\in X_S$, $\|x_v\|\le\\|x\\|_S$. The coordinate vector $e_v$ and every root-to-node path vector have norm one. If $C$ is a finite ancestral subset of $\mathcal T$, then its coordinate projection $P_C$ is contractive and $\\|I-P_C\\|\le2$. For a nonterminal node $t$, the sequence of its child coordinate vectors is weakly null.
  - Explanation: Coordinates are bounded by the norm, paths have unit norm, ancestral projections are contractive, and child vectors are weakly null.
- **claim-segment-start-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-segment-start-tail)): Let $C$ be a finite ancestral subset of $\mathcal T$. If $p\in X_S$ is supported in $C$ and $y\in X_S$ satisfies $y\|_C=0$, then $$\label{sm:s:tail} \max\{\\|p+y\\|_S,\\|p-y\\|_S\}^2 \ge\\|p\\|_S^2+\frac{\\|y\\|_S^2}{64}.$$ The conclusion includes $C=\varnothing$, $p=0$ and $y=0$.
  - Explanation: Matching opposite segments across a finite cut gives a squared midpoint gain in the segment-start space.
- **claim-segment-midpoint-consequence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-s-midpoint-consequence)): For every $x\in X_S$ with $\\|x\\|_S=1$, every $t>0$ and every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a closed finite-codimensional subspace $F\subset X_S$ such that $$\inf_{\substack{y\in F\\\\|y\\|_S=1}} \max\{\\|x+ty\\|_S,\\|x-ty\\|_S\} \ge\sqrt{1+t^2/64}-\varepsilon.$$ In particular, define the maximum form of the asymptotic midpoint modulus by $$\delta^{\max}_{X_S}(t)= \inf_{\\|x\\|_S=1}\sup_{\substack{F\subset X_S\text{ closed}\\\dim(X_S/F)<\infty}} \inf_{\substack{y\in F\\\\|y\\|_S\ge1}} \bigl(\max_{\pm}\\|x\pm ty\\|_S-1\bigr).$$ Then $\delta^{\max}_{X_S}(t)\ge\sqrt{1+t^2/64}-1$.
  - Explanation: The cut estimate gives a lower bound on the maximum asymptotic midpoint modulus at every unit center.
- **claim-incomparable-reflexivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-reflexivity)): Let $Y_{\mathrm I,h}$ be the completion of the arrays supported on $T_h$ in the norm <a href="#sm:i:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:i:norm">[sm:i:norm]</a>. Then $$\frac{\\|g\\|_2}{\sqrt{h+1}} \leq\\|g\\|_{Y_{\mathrm I,h}} \leq\sqrt{h+1}\,\\|g\\|_2, \qquad g\in c_{00}(T_h), \label{sm:i:hilbert}$$ and $$Y_{\mathrm I}=\left(\bigoplus_{h\geq1}Y_{\mathrm I,h}\right)_{\ell_2}.$$ Both $Y_{\mathrm I}$ and $X_{\mathrm I}:=Y_{\mathrm I}^*$ are reflexive.
  - Explanation: The incomparable-segment space is an outer Hilbert sum of Hilbert-equivalent components, so it and its dual are reflexive.
- **claim-incomparable-atom-hull** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-atom-hull)): The unit ball of $X_{\mathrm I}$ is the norm-closed convex hull of the atoms <a href="#sm:i:atoms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:i:atoms">[sm:i:atoms]</a>. In particular, finite linear combinations of the coordinate evaluations are dense in $X_{\mathrm I}$.
  - Explanation: The dual unit ball is the closed convex hull of segment atoms.
- **claim-incomparable-no-auc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-auc-obstruction)): The real reflexive space $X_{\mathrm I}$ admits no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
  - Explanation: Unit root paths with weakly null increments obstruct AUC renorming of the dual space.
- **claim-incomparable-projections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-projections)): For every finite prefix-closed $E$, both $P_E$ and $Q_E$ act contractively on $Y_{\mathrm I}$ and, by adjoints, on $X_{\mathrm I}$. On $X_{\mathrm I}$, $P_E$ has finite rank and retains exactly the coordinates indexed by $E$. For every $x\in X_{\mathrm I}$ and every $\eta>0$, there is such an $E$ with $\\|Q_E x\\|_{\mathrm I}<\eta$.
  - Explanation: Prefix-closed coordinate projections are contractive and tails of any vector can be made small.
- **claim-incomparable-tail-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-tail-estimate)): Let $E\subset\mathcal T$ be finite and prefix closed. If $x,y\in X_{\mathrm I}$ satisfy $$P_E x=x,\qquad \\|x+y\\|_{\mathrm I}\leq1, \qquad \\|x-y\\|_{\mathrm I}\leq1,$$ then $$\\|Q_Ey\\|_{\mathrm I} \leq (\sqrt2+\sqrt6)\sqrt{1-\\|x\\|_{\mathrm I}}. \label{sm:i:tail-bound}$$ In particular, if $Q_Ey=y$, the same bound holds for $\\|y\\|_{\mathrm I}$.
  - Explanation: An arbitrary displacement in a unit lens has tail bounded by the square root of the center deficit.
- **claim-incomparable-separated-midpoints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-i-separated-midpoints)): Let $0<c\leq1$ and let $x,u_j\in X_{\mathrm I}$, $j\in\mathbb N$, satisfy $$\\|x+u_j\\|_{\mathrm I},\ \\|x-u_j\\|_{\mathrm I}\leq1, \qquad \\|u_j-u_k\\|_{\mathrm I}\geq2c\quad(j\ne k).$$ Then, with $K=\sqrt2+\sqrt6$, $$\\|x\\|_{\mathrm I}\leq1-(c/K)^2<1. \label{sm:i:separation-bound}$$
  - Explanation: A common midpoint of an infinite separated family of unit-ball endpoints has norm bounded away from one.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-tree",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a real Banach space. Suppose $0<m\\le M<\\infty$ and $c>0$, and, for every integer $h\\ge1$, $X$ contains a tree $(p_s)_{s\\in\\mathbb N^{\\le h}}$ with $m\\le\\|p_s\\|\\le M$. For every nonterminal $s$, suppose the child increments $d_{s,n}=p_{s^\\frown n}-p_s$ are weakly null as $n\\to\\infty$ and $\\|d_{s,n}\\|\\ge c$. If $N$ is an equivalent norm with $\\alpha\\|\\cdot\\|\\le N\\le\\beta\\|\\cdot\\|$ for constants $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta<\\infty$, then $\\overline\\delta_N(\\alpha c/(2\\beta M))=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For every nonterminal $s$, suppose the child increments $d_{s,n}=p_{s^\\frown n}-p_s$ are weakly null as $n\\to\\infty$ and $\\|d_{s,n}\\|\\ge c$. If $N$ is an equivalent norm with $\\alpha\\|\\cdot\\|\\le N\\le\\beta\\|\\cdot\\|$ for constants $0<\\alpha\\le\\beta<\\infty$, then $\\overline\\delta_N(\\alpha c/(2\\beta M))=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.5"

      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a real Banach space.         "exact_quote": "The space $X$ is reflexive, has separable dual, and its dual is the Hilbert sum of the spaces $(H_n,p_n)$, with pairing $$f(x)=\\sum_n\\langle f_n,x_n\\rangle,\n \\qquad \\|f\\|_*^2=\\sum_np_n(f_n)^2.$$ Every coordinate vector $e_{n,s}$ and every root path $b_{n,s}$ has norm one.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-positive-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C=6(\\sqrt2+1)$. In one component, if $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, and $L_n(x\\pm y)\\le1$, then $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-unit}\n L_n(y)\\le C\\sqrt{1-L_n(x)}.$$ For arbitrary $x,y$ satisfying $P_Ax=x$ and $P_Ay=0$, writing $\\beta=\\max\\{L_n(x+y),L_n(x-y)\\}$ and $r=L_n(x)$, one has $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-scaled}\n L_n(y)^2\\le C^2\\beta(\\beta-r)\\le C^2(\\beta^2-r^2).$$ Consequently, for a finite head projection $P$ on $X$, $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-outer}\n \\|y\\|^2\\le C^2\\bigl(\\|x+y\\|^2+\\|x-y\\|^2-2\\|x\\|^2\\bigr)\n \\quad(Px=x,\\ Py=0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-positive-tail",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C=6(\\sqrt2+1)$. In one component, if $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, and $L_n(x\\pm y)\\le1$, then $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-unit}\n L_n(y)\\le C\\sqrt{1-L_n(x)}.$$ For arbitrary $x,y$ satisfying $P_Ax=x$ and $P_Ay=0$, writing $\\beta=\\max\\{L_n(x+y),L_n(x-y)\\}$ and $r=L_n(x)$, one has $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-scaled}\n L_n(y)^2\\le C^2\\beta(\\beta-r)\\le C^2(\\beta^2-r^2).$$ Consequently, for a finite head projection $P$ on $X$, $$\\label{eq:linear-positive-outer}\n \\|y\\|^2\\le C^2\\bigl(\\|x+y\\|^2+\\|x-y\\|^2-2\\|x\\|^2\\bigr)\n \\quad(Px=x,\\ Py=0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.5"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-linear-subtree-quota",
        "exact_quote": "On a forest of finite height, let $\\mu,\\nu$ be nonnegative finitely supported endpoint masses. For a vertex $v$, let $K_v$ be its descendant set, and put $G(v)=\\mu(K_v)$, $W(v)=\\nu(K_v)$. There is $0\\le\\rho\\le\\mu$ such that $$\\rho(K_v)\\ge\\min\\{G(v),W(v)\\}\\quad\\hbox{for every }v,\n \\qquad \\|\\rho\\|_1\\le\\|\\nu\\|_1.$$ The last inequality also holds separately in each tree of the forest.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-bottom-up-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $0\\le\\varepsilon\\le1$, $P_Ax=x$, $P_Ay=0$, $L_n(x\\pm y)\\le1$, and $L_n(x)\\ge1-\\varepsilon$.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-predictable-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x_0,\\ldots,x_m$ be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Let $P_0$ be a fixed finite initial set, and, for $1\\le i\\le m$, let $P_i$ be a finite initial set measurable with respect to $\\mathcal F_{i-1}$. Suppose, on every history, that $$P_{i-1}\\subseteq P_i\\quad(1\\le i\\le m),\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}x_i\\subseteq P_i\\quad(0\\le i\\le m),\\qquad \\|x_m\\|\\le C.$$ Then, for $\\Delta_i=P_i\\setminus P_{i-1}$, $$\\label{eq:linear-energy-495}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}x_i\\|^2\\le495C^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-predictable-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x_0,\\ldots,x_m$ be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Suppose, on every history, that $$P_{i-1}\\subseteq P_i\\quad(1\\le i\\le m),\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}x_i\\subseteq P_i\\quad(0\\le i\\le m),\\qquad \\|x_m\\|\\le C.$$ Then, for $\\Delta_i=P_i\\setminus P_{i-1}$, $$\\label{eq:linear-energy-495}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}x_i\\|^2\\le495C^2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-331.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $x_0,\\ldots,x_m$ be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Suppose, on every history, that $$P_{i-1}\\subseteq P_i\\quad(1\\le i\\le m),\\qquad\n \\operatorname{supp}x_i\\subseteq P_i\\quad(0\\le i\\le m),\\qquad \\|x_m\\|\\le C.$$ Then, for $\\Delta_i=P_i\\setminus P_{i-1}$, $$\\label{eq:linear-energy-495}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}x_i\\|^2\\le495C^2.$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\subseteq T\\subset T_n$ be finite initial sets, let $z^+,z^-\\in H_n$, and put $z=(z^++z^-)/2$.         "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-sign-energy",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $K$ with the following property. Let $x_t=\\mathbb E_t x_m$, $0\\le t\\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. For $0\\le t<m$, let $A_t\\subseteq D_t$ be finite initial sets measurable with respect to $\\mathcal F_t$, and suppose $D_t\\subseteq A_{t+1}$ whenever $t+1<m$. Put $U_t=D_t\\setminus A_t$. Then $$\\label{eq:linear-sign-energy}\n \\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_{t+1}\\|^2\n \\le K\\left(\\mathbb E\\|x_m\\|^2+\n                  \\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_t\\|^2\\right).$$ No support condition on $x_t$ is imposed.",
        "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-linear-sign-energy",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $K$ with the following property. Let $x_t=\\mathbb E_t x_m$, $0\\le t\\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. For $0\\le t<m$, let $A_t\\subseteq D_t$ be finite initial sets measurable with respect to $\\mathcal F_t$, and suppose $D_t\\subseteq A_{t+1}$ whenever $t+1<m$. Put $U_t=D_t\\setminus A_t$. Then $$\\label{eq:linear-sign-energy}\n \\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_{t+1}\\|^2\n \\le K\\left(\\mathbb E\\|x_m\\|^2+\n                  \\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}\\mathbb E\\|R_{U_t}x_t\\|^2\\right).$$ No support condition on $x_t$ is imposed.",
          "source_id": "PM-331.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute constant $K$ with the following property. Let $x_t=\\mathbb E_t x_m$, $0\\le t\\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Let $A_0\\subseteq\\cdots\\subseteq A_m$ be fixed finite initial sets and put $\\Delta_i=A_i\\setminus A_{i-1}$. Then $$\\label{eq:linear-leakage-1024}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}W_i\\|^2\n \\le1024\\left(\\mathbb E\\|W_m\\|^2+\n              \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}W_{i-1}\\|^2\\right).$$",

      "exact_statement": "Let $W_i=\\mathbb E_iW_m$, $0\\le i\\le m$, be an $X$-valued dyadic martingale. Then $$\\label{eq:linear-leakage-1024}\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}W_i\\|^2\n \\le1024\\left(\\mathbb E\\|W_m\\|^2+\n              \\sum_{i=1}^m\\mathbb E\\|R_{\\Delta_i}W_{i-1}\\|^2\\right).$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-qp-construction",
        "exact_quote": "The spaces $Z_n$ and $X_{\\mathrm q}$ are Banach and reflexive, and finite node support is dense in $X_{\\mathrm q}$.         "source_id": "PM-331.5"

        "anchor": "stmt-qp-rec-all-centers",
        "exact_quote": "For every $x\\in X_{\\mathrm q}$ with $\\|x\\|_{\\mathrm q}=1$ and $0<e\\le1/16$, there is a closed finite-codimensional subspace $Y\\subset X_{\\mathrm q}$ such that $$\\label{qp:rec:complete-bound}\n y\\in Y,\\quad\n \\|x+y\\|_{\\mathrm q},\\|x-y\\|_{\\mathrm q}\\le1+e\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\|y\\|_{\\mathrm q}\\le17(2e)^{1/6}.$$ In particular, for every $t>0$ there is an $e_t>0$, independent of the unit center $x$, for which some closed finite-codimensional $Y$ satisfies $$\\inf_{u\\in S_Y}\n \\max\\{\\|x+tu\\|_{\\mathrm q},\\|x-tu\\|_{\\mathrm q}\\}\\ge1+e_t.$$ Here $S_Y=\\{u\\in Y:\\|u\\|_{\\mathrm q}=1\\}$.         "exact_quote": "The costs are monotone, positively homogeneous and subadditive.         "source_id": "PM-331.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **averaged midpoint modulus** ([source](#eq:averaged-modulus)): For an infinite-dimensional real Banach space $X$ with norm $N$, write $\operatorname{cof}(X)$ for the closed linear subspaces of finite codimension. The averaged midpoint and one-sided moduli used here are $$\begin{aligned} \widehat\delta_N(t) &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(X)} \inf_{\substack{y\in F\\N(y)\ge1}} \left(\frac{N(x+ty)+N(x-ty)}2-1\right), \label{eq:averaged-modulus}\\ \overline\delta_N(t) &=\inf_{N(x)=1}\sup_{F\in\operatorname{cof}(X)} \inf_{\substack{y\in F\\N(y)=1}}\bigl(N(x+ty)-1\bigr), \qquad t>0.\label{eq:auc-modulus} \end{aligned}$$ Asymptotic uniform convexity, abbreviated AUC, means that the second modulus is positive at every positive radius. The first requires a gain in the average of two endpoint norms. Its infimum over directions of norm at least one agrees with the infimum over unit directions: the average on each line is an even convex function of the radius. We retain the larger-direction convention because it is convenient for the quantitative estimates below.
- **quadratic path quotient norm** ([source](#qp:norm)): For an integer $n\ge1$, let $T_n=\mathbb N^{\le n}$, including the empty sequence $o$, with the initial-segment order $\preceq$. Work over the real field. In $H_n=\ell_2(T_n)$ put $$p_t=\sum_{s\preceq t}e_s,\qquad J_n\lambda=\sum_{t\in T_n}\lambda_t p_t \quad(\lambda\in\ell_1(T_n)).$$ The series converges absolutely in $H_n$, since $\\|p_t\\|_2\le\sqrt{n+1}$. Thus $(J_n\lambda)(s)=\sum_{t\succeq s}\lambda_t$ and $\\|J_n\\|\le\sqrt{n+1}$. Define $Z_n$ to be $H_n$ with the norm $$\label{qp:norm} \|z\|_n=\inf_{z=h+J_n\lambda} \bigl(\\|h\\|_2^2+\\|\lambda\\|_1^2\bigr)^{1/2}, \qquad h\in H_n,\quad\lambda\in\ell_1(T_n),$$ and set $$X_{\mathrm q}=\Bigl(\bigoplus_{n\ge1}Z_n\Bigr)_2, \qquad \\|z\\|_{\mathrm q}^2=\sum_n\|z_n\|_n^2.$$ In the notation of Section <a href="#sec:linear-paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:linear-paths">5</a>, $p_t=b_t$, $J_n=B_n$, and $\|z\|_n=Q_n(z)$. To bound midpoint tails, we compare the costs of two endpoint representations with the cost of their average restricted to a finite head.
- **start-mass cost** ([source](#sm:s:cost)): For each integer $n\ge1$, let $\mathcal T_n=\mathbb N^{\le n}$, including its root $\varnothing$. Give nodes their component label, so the trees are disjoint. Write $t\preceq v$ when $t$ is an initial segment of $v$, and $[t,v]=\{s:t\preceq s\preceq v\}$. All scalars in this section are real. For a nonnegative array $m$ on $\mathcal T_n$, define, upwards from the leaves, $$\label{sm:s:cost} q_t(m)=m_t+\Bigl(\sum_{s\text{ child of }t}q_s(m)^2\Bigr)^{1/2}, \qquad M_n(m)=q_{\varnothing}(m).$$ Empty sums are zero; infinite values are allowed in this definition. On the disjoint union $\mathcal T$ of the trees put $$M(m)=\Bigl(\sum_{n\ge1}M_n(m\|_{\mathcal T_n})^2\Bigr)^{1/2}.$$ Thus $M_n$ is a cost for nonnegative start masses, before any vector norm has been defined.
- **incomparable segment norm** ([source](#sm:i:norm)): For a finitely supported real array $g$ on $\mathcal T$, set $$\\|g\\|_{Y_{\mathrm I}} =\sup_{(S_i)\text{ incomparable}} \left(\sum_{i=1}^m \left\|\sum_{v\in S_i}g(v)\right\|^2\right)^{1/2}, \label{sm:i:norm}$$ and let $Y_{\mathrm I}$ be its completion. This is a finite norm on $c_{00}(\mathcal T)$: the triangle inequality follows from the Euclidean triangle inequality, singleton tests separate points, and disjointness bounds every test by $\sum_v\|g(v)\|$. Each coordinate and each finite segment evaluation extends continuously to $Y_{\mathrm I}$. Formula <a href="#sm:i:norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:i:norm">[sm:i:norm]</a> remains valid in the completion. Indeed, the supremum of the extended tests is at most the completion norm and is Lipschitz with constant one; approximation by finitely supported arrays gives the reverse inequality.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-bounded-tree** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-tree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-tree): Iterated selection of a child with multiplicative norm growth along a tall tree, contradicting uniform boundedness. -> vanishing one-sided modulus
- **step-paired-tests** [claim](#stmt-thm-constrained-paired) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-constrained-paired): Clip the relative tail potential into two inward parts and pair them with the two endpoints. -> paired midpoint inequality
- **step-four-spaces** [claim](#stmt-thm-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-constrained-paired-2): Choose the parameter in the paired inequality, then approximate arbitrary centers by finite ones. -> common midpoint bound and no AUC norm
- **step-linear-duality** [claim](#stmt-prop-linear-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linear-duality): Representation estimates plus Hahn–Banach for the dual norm. -> exact duality
- **step-linear-clipping** [claim](#stmt-thm-linear-head-tail-clipping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-linear-head-tail-clipping): Evaluate the head test plus an inward piece on a suitable endpoint. -> clipping estimate
- **step-quadratic-tail** [claim](#stmt-thm-quadratic-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-quadratic-tail): Majority-sign stopped flows against minority mass, with energy loss bounds. -> tail bound
- **step-segment-start-tail** [claim](#stmt-thm-segment-start-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-segment-start-tail): Match opposite segments at the first exit node and charge the discarded part to the later start. -> squared midpoint estimate
- **step-incomparable-tail** [claim](#stmt-sm-i-tail-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-i-tail-estimate): Replace atom coefficients by test sums and control cancellation by shortening segments. -> tail estimate

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-start-cost-recursion**: The start-mass cost of a nonnegative array on a finite tree is obtained bottom-up: each node adds its own mass to the Euclidean combination of its children's values, and the root value is the cost.
- **inv-cost-comparisons**: On a finite-height tree the cost is at least the Euclidean norm of the masses, at most the sum of the level-wise Euclidean norms, and at least every root-to-node path sum of masses.
- **inv-positive-energy**: For nonnegative arrays the squared cost of a sum dominates the sum of the squared costs (checked here on a single finite tree).

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-cost-is-not-norm**: Treat the output of the bottom-up recursion on one fixed array as the norm of a vector in the segment-start space. -> The recursion only prices a given list of start masses; the norm is an infimum over all representations of the vector, so a single evaluation is only an upper bound for it.
- **anti-finite-check-auc**: Conclude from finite-height numerical checks that the infinite-height spaces admit a convexity property that holds after renorming. -> Finite trees give norms equivalent to a Hilbert norm, while the infinite tower of tall trees is exactly what blocks any equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The start-mass cost on a finite tree is an explicit finite bottom-up recursion, so it can be computed and its stated comparison inequalities checked; the infinite-dimensional geometric theorems are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin: node count n, then n parent indices (-1 for the root, parent index smaller than child index), then n nonnegative integer masses.
# PRECONDITIONS: n >= 1; exactly one root at index 0; masses are nonnegative integers.
# OUTPUTS: stdout: the cost of the root, printed as a float.
# TIME: linear in n.
# SPACE: linear in n.
# ARITHMETIC: IEEE double precision floats with square roots; masses are exact integers.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and a nonzero exit status.
import sys
import math


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0])
    par = [int(v) for v in data[1:1 + n]]
    m = [int(v) for v in data[1 + n:1 + 2 * n]]
    ss = [0.0] * n
    q = [0.0] * n
    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
        q[i] = m[i] + math.sqrt(ss[i])
        if par[i] >= 0:
            ss[par[i]] += q[i] * q[i]
    print(repr(q[0]))


main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys\nimport math\n\n\ndef main():\n    toks = open(sys.argv[1]).read().split()\n    n = int(toks[0])\n    par = [int(t) for t in toks[1:1 + n]]\n    m = [int(t) for t in toks[1 + n:1 + 2 * n]]\n    kids = [[] for _ in range(n)]\n    depth = [0] * n\n    for i, p in enumerate(par):\n        if p >= 0:\n            kids[p].append(i)\n            depth[i] = depth[p] + 1\n    sys.setrecursionlimit(10000)\n\n    def q(t):\n        return m[t] + math.sqrt(math.fsum(q(c) ** 2 for c in kids[t]))\n\n    val = q(0)\n    got = float(open(sys.argv[2]).read().strip())\n    exp = float(sys.argv[3])\n    if abs(got - val) > 1e-9 or abs(got - exp) > 1e-9:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    tol = 1e-9\n    if math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in m)) > val + tol:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    levels = {}\n    for i in range(n):\n        levels[depth[i]] = levels.get(depth[i], 0) + m[i] * m[i]\n    if sum(math.sqrt(v) for v in levels.values()) < val - tol:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    acc = [0] * n\n    for i in range(n):\n        acc[i] = m[i] + (acc[par[i]] if par[i] >= 0 else 0)\n        if acc[i] > val + tol:\n            sys.exit(1)\n    sys.exit(0)\n\n\nmain()\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves properties of infinite-dimensional Banach spaces (renorming obstructions, midpoint moduli). It states no program, interface or data flow that could be implemented as a software component. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamics, state update or physical or game model is treated. The infinite trees and spaces serve only to compute convexity moduli. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies no reusable procedure that a practical tool could wrap. Its estimates are existence and inequality statements about norms, not a workflow with inputs and outputs. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results are inequalities and obstructions over completions of infinite sequence spaces. The proofs select near-optimal witnesses and take limits, so there is no finite step-by-step procedure with a cost model. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Trees appear as index sets for coefficients and potentials of a norm, not as a storage or query structure with operations to support. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing is stated about arithmetic kernels, parallel evaluation or numerical precision. The objects are abstract real Banach spaces. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary or security reduction occurs. Norm geometry of tree spaces gives no cryptographic primitive. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning objective, estimator or generalization statement appears. Asymptotic convexity moduli of abstract spaces say nothing about training dynamics. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | On a tree of finite height the norm equals the least additive cost of splitting a vector into a Hilbert part and root-path masses. This is a convex minimisation that could be adapted to hierarchical cost allocation, but the paper gives no solver or complexity. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no processes, messages, failures or consensus questions. Tree coordinates index vectors, not nodes of a network. |
| other (functional analysis and Banach space renorming theory) | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The paper supplies explicit reflexive and separable spaces with positive averaged midpoint moduli that admit no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. Its value is as a theory reference, not as an engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-no-auc-renorming** (counterexample_scope): Positive averaged midpoint bounds in these spaces never yield asymptotic uniform convexity: the one-sided modulus vanishes at an explicit scale under every equivalent norm. Blocked use: ['Assuming a renormed version of any of the four spaces is asymptotically uniformly convex.', 'Using the averaged midpoint bound as a one-sided convexity guarantee for iterates.']
- **limit-radius-range** (hypothesis): The common midpoint bound is stated only for radii in the open interval between zero and one. Blocked use: ['Applying the quantitative bound at radius one or larger.', 'Extrapolating the quadratic gain beyond the stated range.']
- **limit-finite-height-hilbert** (formal_scope): The exact identification of the additive cost with the dual test norm is a finite-height statement for each component; it does not transfer to the infinite-height completion. Blocked use: ['Reading the finite-height duality as a statement about the infinite tree.', 'Using finite-height norm equivalence with a Hilbert norm as evidence for renormability of the infinite tower.']
- **limit-infimum-attainment** (nonconstructive): The path-cost infimum is not assumed to be attained, so no optimal decomposition or solver is provided. Blocked use: ['Presenting the paper as an algorithm that returns an optimal Hilbert-plus-path decomposition.', 'Assuming a minimizer exists when stating complexity or convergence claims.']
- **limit-cost-before-norm** (formal_scope): The start-mass recursion prices a given nonnegative array; the vector norm is an infimum over representations, so one evaluation does not give the norm. Blocked use: ['Reporting the recursion value of a single array as the norm of a vector.', 'Treating the recursion as a norm computation without searching over representations.']
- **limit-unique-ancestors** (hypothesis): All tree models rely on a prefix order in which each node has a unique parent and unique root path. Blocked use: ['Applying root-path decompositions or tree potentials to graphs with several parents per node.', 'Transferring the estimates to reconvergent directed graphs.']
- **limit-center-in-head** (hypothesis): The quadratic tail estimate requires the center to be supported in a finite ancestral head chosen with the center. Blocked use: ['Applying the tail bound to a center with infinite coordinate support without approximation.', 'Choosing the head after the displacement and expecting the same constant.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 331 (`lean/docs/331.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Asymptotic midpoint uniform convexity and unbounded diamond distortion in a reflexive tree space](../../preprints/Asymptotic-midpoint-uniform-convexity-and-unbounded-diamond-distortion-in-a-reflexive-tree-space-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint lenses in segment spaces](../../preprints/Midpoint-lenses-in-segment-spaces-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Distortion of countably branching diamonds from midpoint and tree energies](../../preprints/Diamond-distortion-from-midpoint-and-tree-energies-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Exact asymptotic moduli in a Daugavet subspace of $L_1$](../../preprints/Exact-asymptotic-moduli-in-a-Daugavet-subspace-of-L1-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from bounded tree potentials and path costs](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-bounded-tree-potentials-and-path-costs-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Independent products in real $L_1$: asymptotic midpoint convexity without AUC renormings](../../preprints/Independent-products-in-real-L1-asymptotic-midpoint-convexity-without-AUC-renormings-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
> - [Midpoint convexity from two recursive potentials](../../preprints/Midpoint-convexity-from-two-recursive-potentials-September-27-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper constructs a Banach space whose midpoint geometry is asymptotically uniformly convex even though no equivalent norm is asymptotically uniformly convex in the usual one-sided sense. The formalization proves that the full dual of the specified segment-norm completion is infinite-dimensional, separable, and reflexive; its averaged midpoint modulus is at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/12}-1$ for every $t>0$, while every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity. It also proves that an embedding of a depth-$k$ countably branching diamond has distortion at least $\sqrt{1+k/12}$.
>
> The general-forest and word-forest results use the closed span of the coordinate functionals. Their conclusions retain the finite-ancestor and equivalent-norm hypotheses, including the scale $\alpha/(2\beta)$ when $0<\alpha\le\beta$ and the new norm lies between $\alpha$ and $\beta$ times the original norm; they do not identify that span with the full dual or assert reflexivity for every forest.
>
> The paper controls midpoint lenses in Banach spaces defined by tree-segment norms. The formalization proves the following estimate in both the full dual of the finite-height forest space and the coordinate predual of the infinite-tree space. If $x$ is supported on a finite ancestral set $H$, $R\ge0$, and $\|x+y\|,\|x-y\|\le R$, then the part of $y$ outside $H$ has norm at most $2\sqrt{R^2-\|x\|^2}$.
>
> The formalization also covers selected consequences for averaged midpoint moduli and separated families of vectors, together with reflexivity and renorming obstructions. This scope concerns the segment-space results; the companion results on diamond energies remain separate.
>
> The paper studies embeddings of countably branching diamond graphs into Banach spaces built from tree segments. The formalization proves that every embedding of the depth-$k$ diamond with distortion $D$, at any positive scale, satisfies $1+k/4\le D^2$ in either of two spaces: the full dual of the completed finite-height forest space and the closed coordinate span in the dual of the infinite-tree space. The bound applies to all pairs of vertices and is unconditional.
>
> The formalization also contains selected graph deductions under explicit companion assumptions. Twenty-two of those companion inputs are assumed in these deductions; the diamond distortion bound above does not require them.
>
> The paper exhibits a real $L_1$ subspace with positive averaged midpoint convexity but no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. For every closed infinite-dimensional subspace whose unit ball is precompact in measure and which has the Daugavet property, the formalization computes the moduli at every unit vector: the averaged midpoint modulus is $\max(t/2,t-1)$ and the usual one-sided asymptotic modulus is $\max(0,t-2)$ for every $t>0$. The same formulas hold after taking the infimum over unit vectors, and every equivalent norm fails asymptotic uniform convexity.
>
> The formalization also constructs such a subspace on a countable product of unit intervals and proves that it has the stated measure-precompactness and Daugavet properties, so the modulus formulas apply to an actual example.
>
> The paper constructs Banach spaces from bounded tree potentials and path costs to separate averaged midpoint convexity from asymptotic uniform convexity. For the specified tree-potential completions, the formalization proves completeness, separability, infinite dimension, and an averaged midpoint modulus of at least $\sqrt{1+t^2/4}-1$ for $0<t<1$. No space linearly isomorphic to one of these completions is asymptotically uniformly convex.
>
> The formalization also covers selected path-cost duality and comparison results, clipping and energy estimates, and further renorming obstructions. These retain their stated support, head, and tail hypotheses; the remaining auxiliary assertions of the paper are outside this scope.
>
> The paper forms a real $L_1$ space from the closed span of products of independent multipliers along tree paths. For a positive, nonconstant multiplier of mean one and finite second moment, the formalization proves that this space is infinite-dimensional and has no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm.
>
> For every $t>0$, the averaged midpoint modulus nevertheless has explicit positive lower bounds. If $G$ is a standard real Gaussian, the exponential-multiplier bound is $\frac{7t}{480}\mathbb E[(|G|-20/t)_+]$, where $u_+=\max(u,0)$. For squared-Gaussian multipliers, the bounds are $\frac18\Pr(|G|\ge4\sqrt2(2+\sqrt2)/t)$ and $\frac14\Pr(|G|\ge8\sqrt6/t)$.
>
> The paper constructs three Banach spaces from recursive tree potentials: a root-sum space, a zero-root space, and a joined space. The formalization proves that all three are infinite-dimensional and have no equivalent asymptotically uniformly convex norm. The root-sum and joined spaces have averaged midpoint modulus at least $t^3/128$ for $0<t<1$, and the root-sum modulus is positive for every $t>0$. The zero-root estimate retains its finite ancestral support and tail hypotheses, and the zero-root and joined spaces are reflexive.
>
> The formalization also covers a separated-midpoint result for the joined space and a variable-exponent model with its recursive norm, duality, projections, renorming obstruction, and sixth- and third-order stability estimates. The separation, sequence, support, and head assumptions in these results are essential parts of their statements.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

universe u
open Set
open scoped ENNReal Classical

namespace BoundedTreePotentials

section TestNorms

variable {I : Type*}

structure TestFamily (I : Type*) where
  carrier : Set (I → ℝ)
  zero_mem : (0 : I → ℝ) ∈ carrier
  neg_mem : ∀ f ∈ carrier, -f ∈ carrier
  coeff_bound : ∀ f ∈ carrier, ∀ i, |f i| ≤ 1
  singleton_mem : ∀ i, (fun j => if j = i then (1 : ℝ) else 0) ∈ carrier

instance : CoeSort (TestFamily I) (Type _) := ⟨fun K => K.carrier⟩
instance (K : TestFamily I) : Nonempty K.carrier := ⟨⟨0, K.zero_mem⟩⟩

def finitePairing (f : I → ℝ) : (I →₀ ℝ) →ₗ[ℝ] ℝ :=
  Finsupp.linearCombination ℝ f

def testNorm (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ⨆ f : K, |finitePairing f.val x|

def testSeminorm (K : TestFamily I) : Seminorm ℝ (I →₀ ℝ) := by
  have finitePairing_apply (f : I → ℝ) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      finitePairing f x = ∑ i ∈ x.support, x i * f i := rfl
  have finitePairing_abs_bound (K : TestFamily I) (f : K) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      |finitePairing f.val x| ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i| := by
    rw [finitePairing_apply]
    calc
      |∑ i ∈ x.support, x i * f.val i| ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i * f.val i| :=
        Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _
      _ ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i| := by
        apply Finset.sum_le_sum
        intro i hi
        rw [abs_mul]
        exact mul_le_of_le_one_right (abs_nonneg _) (K.coeff_bound _ f.property i)
  have testNorm_bddAbove (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      BddAbove (range fun f : K => |finitePairing f.val x|) :=
    ⟨∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i|, by rintro _ ⟨f, rfl⟩; exact finitePairing_abs_bound K f x⟩
  have finitePairing_le_testNorm (K : TestFamily I) (f : K) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      |finitePairing f.val x| ≤ testNorm K x := le_ciSup (testNorm_bddAbove K x) f
  have testNorm_nonneg (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) : 0 ≤ testNorm K x :=
    (abs_nonneg _).trans (finitePairing_le_testNorm K ⟨0, K.zero_mem⟩ x)
  have testNorm_le_l1 (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      testNorm K x ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i| := by
    apply ciSup_le
    exact fun f => finitePairing_abs_bound K f x
  have testNorm_zero (K : TestFamily I) : testNorm K 0 = 0 := by
    apply le_antisymm
    · simpa using testNorm_le_l1 K 0
    · exact testNorm_nonneg K 0
  have testNorm_add_le (K : TestFamily I) (x y : I →₀ ℝ) :
      testNorm K (x + y) ≤ testNorm K x + testNorm K y := by
    apply ciSup_le
    intro f
    rw [map_add]
    exact (abs_add_le _ _).trans (add_le_add
      (finitePairing_le_testNorm K f x) (finitePairing_le_testNorm K f y))
  have testNorm_smul_le (K : TestFamily I) (a : ℝ) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      testNorm K (a • x) ≤ ‖a‖ * testNorm K x := by
    apply ciSup_le
    intro f
    rw [map_smul, smul_eq_mul, abs_mul, Real.norm_eq_abs]
    exact mul_le_mul_of_nonneg_left (finitePairing_le_testNorm K f x) (abs_nonneg a)
  exact Seminorm.ofSMulLE (testNorm K) (testNorm_zero K) (testNorm_add_le K) (testNorm_smul_le K)

def TestVector (_ : TestFamily I) := I →₀ ℝ

instance (K : TestFamily I) : AddCommGroup (TestVector K) := inferInstanceAs (AddCommGroup (I →₀ ℝ))
instance (K : TestFamily I) : Module ℝ (TestVector K) := inferInstanceAs (Module ℝ (I →₀ ℝ))
instance (K : TestFamily I) : Norm (TestVector K) := ⟨testNorm K⟩

instance (K : TestFamily I) : NormedAddCommGroup (TestVector K) := by
  have finitePairing_apply (f : I → ℝ) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      finitePairing f x = ∑ i ∈ x.support, x i * f i := rfl
  have finitePairing_abs_bound (K : TestFamily I) (f : K) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      |finitePairing f.val x| ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i| := by
    rw [finitePairing_apply]
    calc
      |∑ i ∈ x.support, x i * f.val i| ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i * f.val i| :=
        Finset.abs_sum_le_sum_abs _ _
      _ ≤ ∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i| := by
        apply Finset.sum_le_sum
        intro i hi
        rw [abs_mul]
        exact mul_le_of_le_one_right (abs_nonneg _) (K.coeff_bound _ f.property i)
  have testNorm_bddAbove (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      BddAbove (range fun f : K => |finitePairing f.val x|) :=
    ⟨∑ i ∈ x.support, |x i|, by rintro _ ⟨f, rfl⟩; exact finitePairing_abs_bound K f x⟩
  have finitePairing_le_testNorm (K : TestFamily I) (f : K) (x : I →₀ ℝ) :
      |finitePairing f.val x| ≤ testNorm K x := le_ciSup (testNorm_bddAbove K x) f
  have finitePairing_single_test (x : I →₀ ℝ) (i : I) :
      finitePairing (fun j => if j = i then (1 : ℝ) else 0) x = x i := by
    classical
    rw [finitePairing_apply]
    simp only [mul_ite, mul_one, mul_zero]
    by_cases hi : i ∈ x.support
    · simp [hi]
    · simp [hi, Finsupp.notMem_support_iff.mp hi]
  have coordinate_le_testNorm (K : TestFamily I) (x : I →₀ ℝ) (i : I) :
      |x i| ≤ testNorm K x := by
    convert finitePairing_le_testNorm K ⟨_, K.singleton_mem i⟩ x using 1
    rw [finitePairing_single_test]
  exact NormedAddCommGroup.ofCore (𝕜 := ℝ) {
    norm_nonneg := apply_nonneg (testSeminorm K)
    norm_smul := map_smul_eq_mul (testSeminorm K)
    norm_triangle := map_add_le_add (testSeminorm K)
    norm_eq_zero_iff := fun x => ⟨by
      intro h
      apply Finsupp.ext
      intro i
      exact abs_nonpos_iff.mp (h ▸ coordinate_le_testNorm K x i), by
      rintro rfl
      exact map_zero (testSeminorm K)⟩ }

instance (K : TestFamily I) : NormedSpace ℝ (TestVector K) where
  norm_smul_le a x := le_of_eq (map_smul_eq_mul (testSeminorm K) a x)

abbrev TestCompletion (K : TestFamily I) := UniformSpace.Completion (TestVector K)

end TestNorms

namespace TreeCalculus

abbrev Node := List ℕ

def potential (f : Node → ℝ) (s : Node) : ℝ := (s.inits.map f).sum

inductive QuadraticKind where
  | sibling | antichain | global
  deriving DecidableEq

def QuadraticGroup : QuadraticKind → Finset Node → Prop
  | .sibling, B => ∃ r, ∀ s ∈ B, ∃ j, s = r ++ [j]
  | .antichain, B => (B : Set Node).Pairwise (fun s t => ¬s <+: t ∧ ¬t <+: s)
  | .global, _ => True

def QuadraticBudget (k : QuadraticKind) (f : Node → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ B : Finset Node, QuadraticGroup k B → ∑ s ∈ B, f s ^ 2 ≤ 1

def CoefficientSupport : QuadraticKind → (Node → ℝ) → Prop
  | .sibling, _ => True
  | .antichain, f => (Function.support f).Finite
  | .global, f => Memℓp f 2

structure IsTreeTest (includeRoot : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) (f : Node → ℝ) : Prop where
  root_zero : includeRoot = false → f [] = 0
  potential_bound : ∀ s, |potential f s| ≤ 1
  quadratic_budget : QuadraticBudget k f
  coefficient_support : CoefficientSupport k f

abbrev TreeCoordinate (includeRoot : Bool) := {s : Node // includeRoot = true ∨ s ≠ []}

def liftCoefficients (r : Bool) (f : TreeCoordinate r → ℝ) (s : Node) : ℝ :=
  if h : r = true ∨ s ≠ [] then f ⟨s, h⟩ else 0

def treeTestFamily (r : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) : TestFamily (TreeCoordinate r) := by
  have potential_root (f : Node → ℝ) : potential f [] = f [] := by simp [potential]
  have potential_child (f : Node → ℝ) (s : Node) (j : ℕ) :
      potential f (s ++ [j]) = potential f s + f (s ++ [j]) := by
    simp [potential, List.inits_append]
  have potential_smul (a : ℝ) (f : Node → ℝ) (s : Node) :
      potential (a • f) s = a * potential f s := by
    induction s using List.reverseRecOn with
    | nil => simp [potential_root]
    | append_singleton s j ih => simp only [potential_child, ih, Pi.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul]; ring
  have quadratic_singleton (k : QuadraticKind) {s : Node} (hs : s ≠ []) :
      QuadraticGroup k {s} := by
    cases k with
    | sibling =>
      cases s using List.reverseRecOn with
      | nil => exact (hs rfl).elim
      | append_singleton s j =>
        refine ⟨s, ?_⟩
        intro t ht
        have ht' : t = s ++ [j] := by simpa using ht
        exact ⟨j, ht'⟩
    | antichain => simp [QuadraticGroup]
    | global => trivial
  have test_coefficient_bound {r : Bool} {k : QuadraticKind} {f : Node → ℝ}
      (hf : IsTreeTest r k f) (s : Node) : |f s| ≤ 1 := by
    by_cases hs : s = []
    · subst s; simpa only [potential_root] using hf.potential_bound []
    · have h := hf.quadratic_budget {s} (quadratic_singleton k hs)
      rw [Finset.sum_singleton] at h
      exact (sq_le_one_iff_abs_le_one (f s)).mp h
  have quadraticBudget_dominate {k : QuadraticKind} {f g : Node → ℝ}
      (hf : QuadraticBudget k f) (h : ∀ s, |g s| ≤ |f s|) : QuadraticBudget k g := by
    intro B hB
    apply le_trans _ (hf B hB)
    apply Finset.sum_le_sum
    intro s hs
    exact (sq_le_sq).mpr (h s)
  have coefficientSupport_dominate {k : QuadraticKind} {f g : Node → ℝ}
      (hf : CoefficientSupport k f) (h : ∀ s, |g s| ≤ |f s|) : CoefficientSupport k g := by
    cases k with
    | sibling => trivial
    | antichain =>
      apply hf.subset
      intro s hs
      change g s ≠ 0 at hs
      change f s ≠ 0
      intro hzero
      have hh := h s
      rw [hzero, abs_zero] at hh
      exact hs (abs_nonpos_iff.mp hh)
    | global =>
      exact (hf.norm).mono (by intro s; simpa only [Real.norm_eq_abs] using h s)
  have zero_isTreeTest (r : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) : IsTreeTest r k 0 := by
    constructor
    · simp
    · intro s
      change |(s.inits.map fun _ => (0 : ℝ)).sum| ≤ 1
      simp
    · intro B hB; simp
    · cases k with
      | sibling => trivial
      | antichain => simp [CoefficientSupport]
      | global => exact zero_memℓp
  have neg_isTreeTest {r : Bool} {k : QuadraticKind} {f : Node → ℝ}
      (hf : IsTreeTest r k f) : IsTreeTest r k (-f) := by
    constructor
    · intro hr; simpa using congrArg Neg.neg (hf.root_zero hr)
    · intro s
      have he : -f = (-1 : ℝ) • f := by ext s; simp
      rw [he, potential_smul, neg_one_mul, abs_neg]
      exact hf.potential_bound s
    · exact quadraticBudget_dominate hf.quadratic_budget (by intro s; simp)
    · exact coefficientSupport_dominate hf.coefficient_support (by intro s; simp)
  have inits_nodup (s : Node) : s.inits.Nodup := by
    induction s using List.reverseRecOn with
    | nil => simp
    | append_singleton s j ih =>
      have he : (s ++ [j]).inits = s.inits ++ [s ++ [j]] := by simp [List.inits_append]
      rw [he, List.nodup_append]
      refine ⟨ih, by simp, ?_⟩
      intro a ha b hb hab
      have hb' : b = s ++ [j] := by simpa using hb
      have hp := ((List.mem_inits a s).mp ha).length_le
      rw [hab, hb', List.length_append, List.length_singleton] at hp
      exact Nat.not_succ_le_self _ hp
  have potential_single (i s : Node) :
      potential (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ)) s = if i <+: s then 1 else 0 := by
    rw [potential, ← List.sum_toFinset _ (inits_nodup s)]
    simp only [Pi.single_apply]
    by_cases h : i <+: s
    · have hm : i ∈ s.inits.toFinset := List.mem_toFinset.mpr ((List.mem_inits i s).mpr h)
      simp [hm, h]
    · have hm : i ∉ s.inits.toFinset := fun hm => h ((List.mem_inits i s).mp (List.mem_toFinset.mp hm))
      simp [hm, h]
  have single_isTreeTest (r : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) {i : Node}
      (hi : r = true ∨ i ≠ []) : IsTreeTest r k (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ)) := by
    constructor
    · intro hr
      have hn : [] ≠ i := by
        rcases hi with hi | hi
        · simp_all
        · exact Ne.symm hi
      exact Pi.single_eq_of_ne hn _
    · intro s
      rw [potential_single]
      split_ifs <;> norm_num
    · intro B hB
      simp only [Pi.single_apply, ite_pow, one_pow, zero_pow (by decide : (2 : ℕ) ≠ 0)]
      by_cases h : i ∈ B <;> simp [h]
    · cases k with
      | sibling => trivial
      | antichain =>
        change (Function.support (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ))).Finite
        exact (Set.finite_singleton i).subset (by intro s hs; simpa [Function.mem_support, Pi.single_apply] using hs)
      | global => exact (lp.single (E := fun _ : Node => ℝ) 2 i (1 : ℝ)).property
  have liftCoefficients_coord {r : Bool} (f : TreeCoordinate r → ℝ) (s : TreeCoordinate r) :
      liftCoefficients r f s.val = f s := by simp [liftCoefficients, s.property]
  have liftCoefficients_zero (r : Bool) : liftCoefficients r 0 = 0 := by
    ext s; simp [liftCoefficients]
  have liftCoefficients_neg (r : Bool) (f : TreeCoordinate r → ℝ) :
      liftCoefficients r (-f) = -liftCoefficients r f := by
    ext s
    by_cases h : r = true ∨ s ≠ [] <;> simp [liftCoefficients, h]
  exact {
    carrier := {f | IsTreeTest r k (liftCoefficients r f)}
    zero_mem := by
      change IsTreeTest r k (liftCoefficients r 0)
      rw [liftCoefficients_zero]
      exact zero_isTreeTest r k
    neg_mem := by
      intro f hf
      change IsTreeTest r k (liftCoefficients r (-f))
      rw [liftCoefficients_neg]
      exact neg_isTreeTest hf
    coeff_bound := by
      intro f hf i
      simpa only [liftCoefficients_coord] using test_coefficient_bound hf i.val
    singleton_mem := by
      rintro ⟨i, hi⟩
      change IsTreeTest r k (liftCoefficients r _)
      convert single_isTreeTest r k hi using 1
      ext s
      by_cases h : r = true ∨ s ≠ []
      · simp [liftCoefficients, h, Pi.single_apply, Subtype.mk.injEq]
      · have hn : s ≠ i := by rintro rfl; exact h hi
        simp [liftCoefficients, h, hn] }

end TreeCalculus

section AsymptoticModuli

variable (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]

structure ClosedFiniteCodim where
  space : Submodule ℝ E
  closed : IsClosed (space : Set E)
  finiteCodim : Module.Finite ℝ (E ⧸ space)

def averagedModulusReal (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ⨅ x : {x : E // ‖x‖=1},⨆ F : ClosedFiniteCodim E,
    ⨅ y : {y : E // y ∈ F.space ∧ 1≤‖y‖},
      (‖x.val+t • y.val‖+‖x.val-t • y.val‖)/((2 : ℕ) : ℝ)-1

def oneSidedModulusReal (t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ⨅ x : {x : E // ‖x‖=1},⨆ F : ClosedFiniteCodim E,
    ⨅ y : {y : E // y ∈ F.space ∧ ‖y‖=1},‖x.val+t • y.val‖-1

def IsAUCReal : Prop := ∀ t : ℝ, 0 < t → 0 < oneSidedModulusReal E t

end AsymptoticModuli

open TreeCalculus in
theorem TreeCalculus.main_counterexample (r : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) :
    (CompleteSpace (TestCompletion (treeTestFamily r k)) ∧
      TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace (TestCompletion (treeTestFamily r k)) ∧
      ¬Module.Finite ℝ (TestCompletion (treeTestFamily r k))) ∧
    (∀ t : ℝ, 0 < t → t < 1 →
      Real.sqrt (1 + t ^ 2 / 4) - 1 ≤
        averagedModulusReal (TestCompletion (treeTestFamily r k)) t) ∧
    (∀ (Y : Type u) [NormedAddCommGroup Y] [NormedSpace ℝ Y],
      (TestCompletion (treeTestFamily r k) ≃L[ℝ] Y) → ¬IsAUCReal Y) := by
  sorry

end BoundedTreePotentials

end

end OAI

simp)\n    · exact coefficientSupport_dominate hf.coefficient_support (by intro s; simp)\n  have inits_nodup (s : Node) : s.inits.Nodup := by\n    induction s using List.reverseRecOn with\n    | nil => simp\n    | append_singleton s j ih =>\n      have he : (s ++ [j]).inits = s.inits ++ [s ++ [j]] := by simp [List.inits_append]\n      rw [he, List.nodup_append]\n      refine ⟨ih, by simp, ?_⟩\n      intro a ha b hb hab\n      have hb' : b = s ++ [j] := by simpa using hb\n      have hp := ((List.mem_inits a s).mp ha).length_le\n      rw [hab, hb', List.length_append, List.length_singleton] at hp\n      exact Nat.not_succ_le_self _ hp\n  have potential_single (i s : Node) :\n      potential (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ)) s = if i <+: s then 1 else 0 := by\n    rw [potential, ← List.sum_toFinset _ (inits_nodup s)]\n    simp only [Pi.single_apply]\n    by_cases h : i <+: s\n    · have hm : i ∈ s.inits.toFinset := List.mem_toFinset.mpr ((List.mem_inits i s).mpr h)\n      simp [hm, h]\n    · have hm : i ∉ s.inits.toFinset := fun hm => h ((List.mem_inits i s).mp (List.mem_toFinset.mp hm))\n      simp [hm, h]\n  have single_isTreeTest (r : Bool) (k : QuadraticKind) {i : Node}\n      (hi : r = true ∨ i ≠ []) : IsTreeTest r k (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ)) := by\n    constructor\n    · intro hr\n      have hn : [] ≠ i := by\n        rcases hi with hi | hi\n        · simp_all\n        · exact Ne.symm hi\n      exact Pi.single_eq_of_ne hn _\n    · intro s\n      rw [potential_single]\n      split_ifs <;> norm_num\n    · intro B hB\n      simp only [Pi.single_apply, ite_pow, one_pow, zero_pow (by decide : (2 : ℕ) ≠ 0)]\n      by_cases h : i ∈ B <;> simp [h]\n    · cases k with\n      | sibling => trivial\n      | antichain =>\n        change (Function.support (Pi.single i (1 : ℝ))).Finite\n        exact (Set.finite_singleton i).subset (by intro s hs;
